01. raw-001 — The Inward Umbrella
Disposition: REVISION_REQUIRED | Eligibility: INELIGIBLE | SFE: 32 | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-001
title: The Inward Umbrella
protagonistSuggestion: Opa
everydayGoal: Stay dry in the rain
primaryObject: Umbrella
normalExpectation: Pressing the button opens the canopy above, creating a dry shield.
impossibleRule: The umbrella opens downward, wrapping around the user's body instead of above it, and always migrates to cover the lowest point.
visibleTrigger: Opa presses the open button under rain; the canopy blooms down over his arm and torso, leaving his head wet.
whyThisObject: An umbrella's entire purpose is to open into a dome over your head; inverting that dome makes it wrap you in wet fabric while rain still hits your exposed head.
goalRuleRelationship: The action meant to create dry overhead space instead traps the body and concentrates rain onto the uncovered head, directly preventing dryness.
frameZeroDescription: Opa stands in rain with a closed umbrella raised; the button has been pressed but the canopy has opened downward around his arm and upper body, his head exposed to downpour.
Beat 1
  Action: Opa pulls the inverted canopy off his head and points the handle skyward, pressing button again to close.
  Visible consequence: The wet fabric slides up his arm and seals around his neck like a collar, while rain soaks his exposed face.
  New information: The umbrella can't be removed by pulling; it migrates to the highest point of his body.
Beat 2
  Action: Opa crouches low to the ground, hoping the umbrella will slide up to his raised hand.
  Visible consequence: The canopy promptly engulfs his crouched back and legs, leaving only his feet sticking out, while rain pools on the fabric.
  New information: The umbrella always moves to cover the lowest body part, not the highest.
Beat 3
  Action: Opa lies flat on the ground, spreading arms and legs to prevent any body part from being lowest.
  Visible consequence: The umbrella flattens over his entire body like a tarp, but water runs off the edges and forms a puddle around him, soaking him from below.
  New information: Being covered doesn't stop water; it collects and soaks from ground contact.
Beat 4
  Action: Opa rolls sideways toward a bus shelter, hoping to escape the rain while still covered.
  Visible consequence: The umbrella rolls with him, wrapping around his torso like a soggy cocoon, and his head remains in the rain as he bumps into the shelter wall.
  New information: The umbrella's coverage deflects all rain away from any part it touches, concentrating rain onto uncovered head.
Beat 5
  Action: Opa sits up inside the shelter, folds the umbrella manually by hand, and uses it as a normal hat.
  Visible consequence: The umbrella stays open upside-down over his head, collecting rainwater that pours down his face inside the shelter.
  New information: The impossible orientation persists even when manually adjusted; any use creates a personal rain cloud.
finalPayoff: The umbrella stays open upside-down over his head, collecting rainwater that pours down his face inside the shelter.
suggestedPayoffFamily: inversion persists despite manual correction
loopPotential: Each new body position causes the umbrella to relocate to the lowest point, so Opa keeps trying to out-think the wrap but only changes which part gets covered.
environment: Rainy city sidewalk
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: The protective function is reversed into a wet trap; visually clear because a downward-opening umbrella is instantly wrong for staying dry.

02. raw-002 — Self-Squeezing Toothpaste
Disposition: REJECTED | Eligibility: INELIGIBLE | SFE: 18 | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-002
title: Self-Squeezing Toothpaste
protagonistSuggestion: Kiko
everydayGoal: Brush teeth
primaryObject: Toothpaste tube
normalExpectation: Squeezing tube dispenses paste onto brush.
impossibleRule: The tube squeezes itself from the bottom when cap is off, but any paste exiting immediately crawls back inside the nozzle when you squeeze manually.
visibleTrigger: Kiko removes cap and paste oozes out on its own; when Kiko squeezes, the paste retracts.
whyThisObject: Toothpaste is meant to be pushed out by external pressure; reversing cause and effect makes the tube act like a living creature controlling its contents.
goalRuleRelationship: Every attempt to get paste onto brush results in either uncontrollable overflow or instant reattachment to tube, so brushing never gets paste.
frameZeroDescription: Kiko holds a toothbrush ready while a toothpaste tube on the counter is already oozing a snake of paste upward; Kiko's other hand reaches to squeeze, but the paste begins sliding back into the nozzle.
Beat 1
  Action: Kiko grabs the tube and squeezes hard to force paste onto brush.
  Visible consequence: The ribbon of paste outside retracts fully into the nozzle, leaving brush dry.
  New information: Manual squeezing causes paste to be sucked back, opposite of normal.
Beat 2
  Action: Kiko holds the tube upright and waits for it to ooze on its own, then brings brush underneath.
  Visible consequence: Paste drips onto the brush but continues flowing, wrapping around bristles and gluing them into a blob.
  New information: The tube's self-squeeze never stops, creating an endless stream.
Beat 3
  Action: Kiko yanks the brush away to break the paste stream.
  Visible consequence: The paste string stretches and snaps, flinging the blob back onto Kiko's nose; the tube continues to ooze a new strand.
  New information: Breaking the stream causes projectile paste, and the tube refills itself instantly.
Beat 4
  Action: Kiko caps the tube and uses a finger to scrape paste from his nose onto the brush.
  Visible consequence: The tube starts shaking and expands, popping the cap off and spraying paste across the mirror.
  New information: The tube cannot be stopped by capping; pressure builds and releases violently.
Beat 5
  Action: Kiko hides the tube in a drawer and quickly rubs the brush with the paste already on it.
  Visible consequence: The drawer rattles, the tube rockets out, and a fresh paste snake lands directly on Kiko's tongue.
  New information: The tube actively targets Kiko's mouth, ensuring the paste reaches the goal but not via the brush.
finalPayoff: The drawer rattles, the tube rockets out, and a fresh paste snake lands directly on Kiko's tongue.
suggestedPayoffFamily: absurd success via object's own action
loopPotential: Kiko might try closing the drawer, using a different tube, or catching the paste, but the tube always ejects paste onto his tongue exactly when he gives up.
environment: Bathroom sink and mirror
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Toothpaste self-dispensing and refilling is a direct function inversion; the tube controls the paste rather than the user.

03. raw-003 — Spiraling Shopping Cart
Disposition: REJECTED | Eligibility: INELIGIBLE | SFE: 19 | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-003
title: Spiraling Shopping Cart
protagonistSuggestion: Mimi
everydayGoal: Push cart through supermarket to checkout
primaryObject: Shopping cart
normalExpectation: All four wheels swivel and roll straight when pushed.
impossibleRule: Each wheel locks in alternating directions every time the cart moves, causing cart to travel in tightening spirals toward the store entrance.
visibleTrigger: Mimi pushes forward, cart veers left and circles back.
whyThisObject: Cart wheels are meant to provide straight mobility; locking their directions converts steering into a chaotic orbit.
goalRuleRelationship: The push intended to move cart forward instead causes a spiral away from checkout, preventing reaching the goal.
frameZeroDescription: Mimi stands behind a shopping cart in an aisle, hands on handle, but all four wheels are pointed in different fixed angles; the cart has already begun a curved path back toward the entrance doors.
Beat 1
  Action: Mimi pushes harder and straightens handle to go down aisle.
  Visible consequence: The cart jerks right, then left, tracing a spiral that wraps around a cereal display.
  New information: Force increases turning radius instability, not direction control.
Beat 2
  Action: Mimi tries to pull the cart backward, hoping the wheels unlock.
  Visible consequence: The cart spins in place like a top, cans rattling, then accelerates forward in a new spiral toward the produce section.
  New information: Backward motion resets wheel angles to new random locks, worsening path.
Beat 3
  Action: Mimi leans over and tries to rotate one wheel by hand while holding cart steady.
  Visible consequence: The other three wheels snap to new positions, causing the cart to lurch sideways and knock over a stack of oranges.
  New information: Wheel directions are interdependent; adjusting one changes all.
Beat 4
  Action: Mimi abandons pushing and drags the cart along the floor by its front corner.
  Visible consequence: The cart tilts and rolls on two wheels, then slams down and spirals toward the automatic entrance, triggering doors to open.
  New information: The cart is stable only when moving sideways, but that still leads to exit.
Beat 5
  Action: Mimi lets go of the cart and runs toward checkout, hoping to grab items and leave.
  Visible consequence: The cart, still spiraling, bumps into Mimi from behind, pushing her through the checkout line and out the exit.
  New information: The cart's uncontrollable motion actively pursues Mimi, forcing her out of the store without paying.
finalPayoff: The cart, still spiraling, bumps into Mimi from behind, pushing her through the checkout line and out the exit.
suggestedPayoffFamily: goal reached wrongly / environmental consequence
loopPotential: Any attempt to steer or adjust wheels only creates a new spiral, and the cart inevitably herds Mimi toward the exit.
environment: Supermarket aisle with checkout and automatic doors
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Wheels are for straight motion; reversing that into locked spirals is instantly intuitive and blocks shopping goal.

04. raw-004 — Sweep-Back Broom
Disposition: REVISION_REQUIRED | Eligibility: INELIGIBLE | SFE: 38 | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-004
title: Sweep-Back Broom
protagonistSuggestion: Arda
everydayGoal: Sweep dirt into a dustpan
primaryObject: Broom
normalExpectation: Sweeping moves dirt forward into a pile.
impossibleRule: Dirt swept forward reappears in its original location exactly two seconds later, while the broom's bristles leave a clean trail that slowly fills back.
visibleTrigger: Arda sweeps a pile to the dustpan, turns to get pan, and the dirt is already back where it started.
whyThisObject: Broom bristles are meant to displace particles; if the displacement is temporary, sweeping becomes a time-delayed loop.
goalRuleRelationship: The action of sweeping creates only a brief illusion of cleanliness; dirt returns before it can be collected, preventing the goal.
frameZeroDescription: Arda stands with broom in mid-sweep, a neat pile near dustpan; behind him, the original dirt patch is slowly reappearing on the floor.
Beat 1
  Action: Arda sweeps quickly and immediately tilts dustpan to catch pile.
  Visible consequence: As he tilts pan, the pile dissolves and dirt races back to the original spot, leaving pan empty.
  New information: The delay is exactly two seconds, and any pause lets dirt escape.
Beat 2
  Action: Arda sweeps while holding dustpan in the other hand, moving both together.
  Visible consequence: Dirt enters the pan but then flies out backward through the bristles, returning to start.
  New information: The broom's bristles repel dirt when the pan is close, so dirt cannot be trapped.
Beat 3
  Action: Arda turns the broom over and uses the handle to push dirt like a rake.
  Visible consequence: The dirt stays put for a full three seconds, then jumps back even faster, scattering across a wider area.
  New information: Using a non-bristle part changes the delay to three seconds but increases speed of return.
Beat 4
  Action: Arda sweeps dirt into an open shoebox and quickly closes the lid.
  Visible consequence: The box shakes, dirt bursts out the seams, and returns to the original spot, leaving box empty.
  New information: Containment fails; dirt always phases back to start regardless of barrier.
Beat 5
  Action: Arda gives up and simply walks away, leaving the broom on the floor.
  Visible consequence: The broom starts sweeping itself in small arcs, and the dirt forms one neat pile in the center of the room.
  New information: The broom can only sweep effectively when no one is trying to collect the dirt.
finalPayoff: The broom starts sweeping itself in small arcs, and the dirt forms one neat pile in the center of the room.
suggestedPayoffFamily: role reversal / object does task when abandoned
loopPotential: Arda may return to collect, but then dirt scatters; the broom only succeeds when unattended.
environment: Kitchen floor
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Broom's cleaning function is delayed and reversed; the object works only by itself, creating a clear causal joke.

05. raw-005 — Vacuum Blows
Disposition: REVISION_REQUIRED | Eligibility: INELIGIBLE | SFE: 33 | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-005
title: Vacuum Blows
protagonistSuggestion: Luca
everydayGoal: Clean crumbs from carpet
primaryObject: Vacuum cleaner
normalExpectation: Vacuum sucks air and debris into bag.
impossibleRule: The vacuum blows air outward instead of sucking, scattering dust and crumbs toward the user's face and across the room.
visibleTrigger: Luca turns it on, crumbs fly up and coat his glasses.
whyThisObject: Vacuum's core function is negative pressure; reversing polarity turns cleaning into a debris storm.
goalRuleRelationship: The action meant to remove crumbs instead propels them everywhere, making the room dirtier and preventing cleaning.
frameZeroDescription: Luca pushes a vacuum over a carpet, but a cloud of dust and crumbs is erupting from the nozzle, covering his shoes and floating upward.
Beat 1
  Action: Luca checks the bag and points nozzle away, turning it on again.
  Visible consequence: A blast of air shoots out, knocking over a plant and spraying soil across the floor.
  New information: The motor polarity is reversed, so it's now a leaf blower.
Beat 2
  Action: Luca reverses the vacuum's hose, attaching the other end to the front.
  Visible consequence: The machine still blows, but now it sends air into its own bag, inflating it like a balloon until it pops.
  New information: Reversing the hose doesn't change airflow; it just pressurizes the containment.
Beat 3
  Action: Luca holds the vacuum bag shut with his hand and tilts the nozzle toward a window.
  Visible consequence: The bag inflates, lifts the vacuum off the floor, and starts flying around the room, spraying dust in circles.
  New information: The trapped air turns the vacuum into a thruster, causing uncontrolled flight.
Beat 4
  Action: Luca unplugs the vacuum to stop the chaos.
  Visible consequence: The vacuum falls to the floor and lets out a final puff of dust that settles exactly back onto the carpet.
  New information: Without power, the debris returns to its original position due to the reversed air pressure.
Beat 5
  Action: Luca plugs it back in, but this time turns the power dial to 'max' hoping it will suck.
  Visible consequence: The vacuum rockets forward, dragging Luca across the carpet and out the door, leaving a trail of sucked-up crumbs behind.
  New information: Max power reverses thrust direction, so the vacuum now sucks only when moving backward, dragging the user.
finalPayoff: The vacuum rockets forward, dragging Luca across the carpet and out the door, leaving a trail of sucked-up crumbs behind.
suggestedPayoffFamily: cause/result inversion via motion
loopPotential: Each correction flips the airflow direction, but always in the opposite of what Luca expects, so he is alternately blown or dragged.
environment: Living room
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Vacuum's suction reversed is instantly wrong; causing it to become a thruster adds absurd consequence.

06. raw-006 — Mirrored Doorway
Disposition: REVISION_REQUIRED | Eligibility: INELIGIBLE | SFE: 33 | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-006
title: Mirrored Doorway
protagonistSuggestion: Noah
everydayGoal: Enter the kitchen from hallway
primaryObject: Door
normalExpectation: Opening a door leads to the room on the other side.
impossibleRule: Whichever side of the door Noah opens, he steps into the same hallway but mirrored left-right.
visibleTrigger: Noah opens kitchen door and sees hallway behind him without turning around.
whyThisObject: A door is a threshold; if it teleports you back but mirrored, the transition is impossible to complete.
goalRuleRelationship: The act of crossing to the kitchen instead returns him to the hallway, preventing the goal.
frameZeroDescription: Noah holds a door open; through the doorway is a reversed hallway identical to the one behind him, while a kitchen is visible in the mirror behind him.
Beat 1
  Action: Noah steps through the doorway quickly, expecting to enter kitchen.
  Visible consequence: He lands in a mirrored hallway, with door now behind him showing the kitchen as a reflection.
  New information: Passing through flips his orientation and swaps which side is real.
Beat 2
  Action: Noah backs through the doorway, hoping to reverse the mirror effect.
  Visible consequence: He ends up in the original hallway but now the door is on his left instead of right, and the kitchen is behind him.
  New information: Backing through does not cancel the flip; it just reorients his body.
Beat 3
  Action: Noah tries to open the door from the mirrored hallway's other side.
  Visible consequence: The door opens to an identical mirrored hallway nested inside, and Noah sees multiple copies of himself standing in doorways.
  New information: Each crossing creates a new mirrored copy of the same hallway, like an infinite recursion.
Beat 4
  Action: Noah holds the door open with one hand and reaches through to touch the kitchen counter he can see in the reflection.
  Visible consequence: His arm disappears through the doorway and emerges from a door behind him, tapping his own shoulder.
  New information: Space is folded so any object poked through wraps around to the starting side.
Beat 5
  Action: Noah closes the door entirely, then opens it again while looking at his feet.
  Visible consequence: He steps through and finds himself in the kitchen, but everything is mirrored—cabinets open the wrong way, and the clock runs backward.
  New information: The door finally allows entry, but the entire kitchen's handedness is reversed, so using it remains confusing.
finalPayoff: He steps through and finds himself in the kitchen, but everything is mirrored—cabinets open the wrong way, and the clock runs backward.
suggestedPayoffFamily: spatial rule change / goal reached with side effect
loopPotential: Every additional door in the kitchen may also be mirrored, leading Noah to question every threshold.
environment: Hallway and kitchen doorway
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Door as threshold is subverted by self-mirroring; intuitive because you see same hallway where kitchen should be.

07. raw-007 — Backward Alarm Clock
Disposition: REVISION_REQUIRED | Eligibility: INELIGIBLE | SFE: 46 | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-007
title: Backward Alarm Clock
protagonistSuggestion: Opa
everydayGoal: Wake up at 7 AM after setting alarm
primaryObject: Alarm clock
normalExpectation: Alarm rings at set time; hands move forward.
impossibleRule: The clock's hands move backward whenever the alarm is set, so time appears to run in reverse; the alarm rings only when the hands return to midnight.
visibleTrigger: Opa sets alarm for 7:00, the hour hand starts cranking counterclockwise.
whyThisObject: Clock is meant to measure forward time; reversing hands makes scheduled wake-up impossible.
goalRuleRelationship: Setting the alarm to a future time causes the clock to move backward away from that time, so the alarm will never ring at 7 AM.
frameZeroDescription: Opa sits up in bed, staring at a bedside clock whose hour hand is moving backward past 6, while the alarm hand points to 7.
Beat 1
  Action: Opa manually winds the clock forward to 6:59, expecting the alarm soon.
  Visible consequence: As soon as he releases, the hands spin backward rapidly to 3:00.
  New information: Manual forward adjustment is undone by the reverse mechanism.
Beat 2
  Action: Opa turns the alarm off and just watches the clock to see what time it shows.
  Visible consequence: The hands freeze, then twitch backward one minute for every real second that passes.
  New information: Without alarm set, the clock still runs backward but slower.
Beat 3
  Action: Opa sets the alarm for a time that already appears behind the current hand position, like 2:00, hoping it will trigger quickly.
  Visible consequence: The alarm rings immediately, but the hands jump forward to noon, and the ringing reverses itself into a silent vacuum of sound.
  New information: Triggering the alarm causes time to jump forward to noon and sound to invert.
Beat 4
  Action: Opa unplugs the clock and uses his phone to check the actual time.
  Visible consequence: The clock's display reappears on his phone screen, showing the same backward time, and the phone's alarm icon turns into an hourglass.
  New information: The clock's impossible rule spreads to other time-keeping devices.
Beat 5
  Action: Opa gives up and falls back asleep.
  Visible consequence: The clock chimes twelve times in reverse, and Opa wakes up at 7 AM, but the clock reads 7 PM.
  New information: The backward time eventually catches up to the correct wake hour, but day and night are swapped.
finalPayoff: The clock chimes twelve times in reverse, and Opa wakes up at 7 AM, but the clock reads 7 PM.
suggestedPayoffFamily: delayed consequence / goal reached wrongly via causal inversion
loopPotential: If Opa trusts the clock, he'll sleep through day; if he ignores it, he wakes on time but distrusts all clocks.
environment: Bedroom with bedside clock
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Clock's directional time reversal directly prevents scheduled waking; the impossible rule is visually obvious with backward hands.

08. raw-008 — Neighbor's TV Remote
Disposition: REJECTED | Eligibility: INELIGIBLE | SFE: 18 | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-008
title: Neighbor's TV Remote
protagonistSuggestion: Kiko
everydayGoal: Change channel to watch favorite show
primaryObject: TV remote
normalExpectation: Pressing buttons changes your own TV channel.
impossibleRule: The remote's signal always targets the neighbor's television through the wall, changing their channel while your TV stays off.
visibleTrigger: Kiko presses power; neighbor yells, but Kiko's TV remains black.
whyThisObject: Remote is designed to control one specific TV; misdirecting signal to wrong TV makes it useless for your goal.
goalRuleRelationship: The intended action of controlling your TV instead controls someone else's, so your show cannot be selected.
frameZeroDescription: Kiko points remote at his dark TV, but through the apartment wall, the neighbor's TV screen is visibly flickering channels, and the neighbor's shouting bubble appears.
Beat 1
  Action: Kiko moves closer to his TV, pressing power repeatedly.
  Visible consequence: The neighbor's TV volume increases to maximum, shaking the wall, while Kiko's screen stays off.
  New information: Distance doesn't matter; the remote's signal is hardwired to the neighbor's set.
Beat 2
  Action: Kiko covers the remote's IR emitter with his hand, hoping to block scatter.
  Visible consequence: The neighbor's TV turns off and on rapidly, causing a strobe effect through the window.
  New information: Blocking the emitter still sends an encoded signal that only the neighbor's TV understands.
Beat 3
  Action: Kiko opens his TV's manual buttons and tries to change channel directly.
  Visible consequence: The neighbor's TV switches to a karaoke channel, and the neighbor starts singing loudly, while Kiko's TV ignores the manual buttons.
  New information: Any input intended for Kiko's TV is transferred to the neighbor's TV due to a crossed signal wire.
Beat 4
  Action: Kiko unplugs his TV to 'reset' the remote link.
  Visible consequence: The neighbor's TV turns off and then on, now showing Kiko's living room via a hidden camera feed.
  New information: The link is now bidirectional; the neighbor's TV displays Kiko's actions.
Beat 5
  Action: Kiko goes next door to ask the neighbor to change their channel to Kiko's show.
  Visible consequence: The neighbor agrees, but when they press their own remote, Kiko's TV turns on and changes to a cooking show.
  New information: The cross-wiring is symmetrical: neighbor's remote controls Kiko's TV.
finalPayoff: The neighbor agrees, but when they press their own remote, Kiko's TV turns on and changes to a cooking show.
suggestedPayoffFamily: role reversal / bidirectional transfer
loopPotential: Kiko could swap remotes with neighbor, but that might swap their TVs entirely, leading to endless confusion.
environment: Apartment living room with thin wall to neighbor
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Remote's targeted control is rerouted to wrong device; visually funny when neighbor reacts and your screen stays dark.

09. raw-009 — Ceiling Pancake
Disposition: REVISION_REQUIRED | Eligibility: INELIGIBLE | SFE: 28 | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-009
title: Ceiling Pancake
protagonistSuggestion: Mimi
everydayGoal: Flip a pancake
primaryObject: Frying pan
normalExpectation: A quick wrist flip sends pancake up and back into pan.
impossibleRule: The pan exerts a vertical launch force so strong that any food flies upward and sticks to the ceiling; the pan then becomes too slippery to hold.
visibleTrigger: Mimi flips the pancake; it rockets to ceiling and stays.
whyThisObject: Pan's function is to toss food for cooking; if toss becomes permanent adhesion, cooking is impossible.
goalRuleRelationship: The flipping action meant to turn the pancake instead removes it from the pan and glues it overhead, preventing eating.
frameZeroDescription: Mimi holds a frying pan, looking up; a pancake is stuck flat on the ceiling, and a second pancake arcs upward from the pan.
Beat 1
  Action: Mimi scrapes the pancake off the ceiling with a spatula, then tries a gentler flip.
  Visible consequence: The pancake still leaps up and splats on ceiling, sticking even harder.
  New information: The launch force is independent of wrist strength.
Beat 2
  Action: Mimi holds a plate over the pan and flips, hoping to catch the pancake.
  Visible consequence: The pancake punches through the plate, sending shards flying, and both pancake and plate fragments stick to ceiling.
  New information: The launched food has enough force to penetrate obstacles.
Beat 3
  Action: Mimi turns off the stove and waits for the pancake to fall.
  Visible consequence: The pancake slowly peels off the ceiling and falls back into the pan, but immediately turns black and smokes.
  New information: The pancake's fall is controlled by cooking timer, not gravity; it returns overcooked.
Beat 4
  Action: Mimi tries to cook without flipping, using a lid to steam.
  Visible consequence: The pan's handle starts vibrating, and all the batter in the pan spontaneously flips itself to the ceiling in one big sheet.
  New information: The pan's flipping rule applies to any batter inside, even without a wrist motion.
Beat 5
  Action: Mimi gives up and turns off the stove, leaving the pan on the counter.
  Visible consequence: The pan itself flips upward, sticks to the ceiling, and the pancake falls onto Mimi's head.
  New information: The pan's upward launch now applies to itself, completing the role reversal.
finalPayoff: The pan itself flips upward, sticks to the ceiling, and the pancake falls onto Mimi's head.
suggestedPayoffFamily: role reversal / object takes action
loopPotential: Any cooking utensil in this kitchen may also launch upward, forcing Mimi to cook with ceiling-mounted tools.
environment: Kitchen with stove
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Pan's flip function is exaggerated into ceiling adhesion; visually clear and blocks eating.

10. raw-010 — Kettle Rain Cloud
Disposition: REJECTED | Eligibility: INELIGIBLE | SFE: 24 | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-010
title: Kettle Rain Cloud
protagonistSuggestion: Arda
everydayGoal: Make a cup of tea
primaryObject: Kettle
normalExpectation: Kettle boils water, steam escapes, hot water pours into cup.
impossibleRule: Boiling water evaporates into a small rain cloud that follows the kettle, and the rain falls only when you try to pour, often into your face or elsewhere.
visibleTrigger: Arda boils kettle, a mini cloud forms above it; when he tilts, rain falls on his hand.
whyThisObject: Kettle's steam should dissipate; if it condenses into a mobile cloud, it steals the water before it can be poured.
goalRuleRelationship: The water never stays liquid to pour; it becomes a cloud that rains unpredictably, preventing tea.
frameZeroDescription: Arda holds a boiling kettle with a tiny gray cloud hovering just above the spout; small raindrops fall onto his sleeve.
Beat 1
  Action: Arda waves away the cloud and quickly pours into a cup.
  Visible consequence: The cloud darts under the stream, catching all the water as it pours, leaving the cup dry.
  New information: The cloud can intercept the stream, so water becomes rain before reaching cup.
Beat 2
  Action: Arda holds the cup over the kettle spout to catch water before cloud intercepts.
  Visible consequence: The cloud squeezes through the spout and rains inside the cup, but the water immediately evaporates back up to the cloud.
  New information: Any liquid water in the cup is reabsorbed by the cloud due to its attachment to the kettle's heat.
Beat 3
  Action: Arda puts the kettle in the refrigerator to cool the cloud down.
  Visible consequence: The cloud starts snowing, covering Arda's hand and the counter with frost, while the kettle remains hot.
  New information: Cooling the environment changes the cloud's precipitation but not its stealing behavior.
Beat 4
  Action: Arda tilts the kettle and holds an umbrella over his head.
  Visible consequence: The cloud expands into a full room-wide rainstorm, soaking everything except the cup.
  New information: The cloud's size scales with attempts to shield from it.
Beat 5
  Action: Arda unplugs the kettle and uses a microwave to heat water directly.
  Visible consequence: The cloud follows the kettle, which now flies off the counter and hovers over the microwave, raining into it and shorting the appliance.
  New information: The cloud is bonded to the kettle object, not the heating method, and it actively prevents any other water heating.
finalPayoff: The cloud follows the kettle, which now flies off the counter and hovers over the microwave, raining into it and shorting the appliance.
suggestedPayoffFamily: environmental takeover / self-created obstacle
loopPotential: Arda might use a different kettle, but the cloud may transfer to that one, chasing any boiling attempt.
environment: Kitchen counter
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Kettle's steam condensing into a thieving rain cloud is intuitive and directly blocks tea.

11. raw-011 — Dirtying Soap
Disposition: REVISION_REQUIRED | Eligibility: INELIGIBLE | SFE: 36 | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-011
title: Dirtying Soap
protagonistSuggestion: Luca
everydayGoal: Wash hands clean
primaryObject: Soap bar
normalExpectation: Rubbing soap with water removes dirt.
impossibleRule: Soap transfers dirt from the sink onto hands, making them grimier with every scrub; when rinsed, the soap laughs and re-dirties the water.
visibleTrigger: Luca rubs soap, hands turn black.
whyThisObject: Soap's chemical action is to lift dirt; reversing it makes soap a dirt magnet that deposits grime.
goalRuleRelationship: The act of washing applies more dirt, so hands never become clean.
frameZeroDescription: Luca stands at sink with soapy hands, but his hands are covered in dark grime, and the soap bar is pristine white while black suds flow down the drain.
Beat 1
  Action: Luca rinses hands thoroughly under water.
  Visible consequence: The water runs black, but his hands remain black, and the soap bar floats over and touches his elbow, transferring more grime.
  New information: Rinsing does not remove soap's dirt; the soap actively reattaches to skin.
Beat 2
  Action: Luca uses a nail brush to scrub the grime off.
  Visible consequence: The brush strokes push the grime deeper into his skin, making his hands look like charcoal.
  New information: Any friction with the grime helps it penetrate, not remove.
Beat 3
  Action: Luca switches to liquid soap from a bottle.
  Visible consequence: The liquid soap pours out as black goo and coats the bar soap, which then leaps into Luca's pocket, staining his clothes.
  New information: All soaps in the environment are contaminated and transfer grime to any surface.
Beat 4
  Action: Luca dries his hands with a paper towel.
  Visible consequence: The towel tears away a layer of grime but reveals a second layer of different colored dirt underneath.
  New information: The dirt is layered, and removing the top layer just exposes the next.
Beat 5
  Action: Luca gives up and leaves the bathroom, hands black.
  Visible consequence: The soap bar follows him, bouncing along the floor, and wherever it touches, grime appears, making a trail.
  New information: The soap's dirt-creating ability extends beyond hands to the environment.
finalPayoff: The soap bar follows him, bouncing along the floor, and wherever it touches, grime appears, making a trail.
suggestedPayoffFamily: delayed consequence / environmental spread
loopPotential: Luca can try other cleaning products, but they may all become dirt generators due to the soap's influence.
environment: Bathroom sink
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Soap's cleaning action reversed into dirt application is a direct function inversion with messy visual payoff.

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-012
title: Wet-Transfer Towel
protagonistSuggestion: Noah
everydayGoal: Dry off after shower
primaryObject: Towel
normalExpectation: Towel absorbs water from body.
impossibleRule: Towel absorbs water from any wet surface but immediately releases it onto any dry surface it touches next, including Noah.
visibleTrigger: Noah wraps towel around waist; water pours out onto the floor.
whyThisObject: Towel is meant to hold water; if it discharges instantly, drying becomes a game of wet transfer.
goalRuleRelationship: The action of drying with towel transfers water back onto body or surroundings, so Noah never stays dry.
frameZeroDescription: Noah stands in bathroom with towel around waist, but water is streaming from the towel onto his legs and pooling on the floor.
Beat 1
  Action: Noah squeezes the towel over the bathtub to drain it.
  Visible consequence: The towel releases a torrent, then immediately reabsorbs water from the air, swelling up.
  New information: The towel can reabsorb water from humidity, so squeezing doesn't empty it.
Beat 2
  Action: Noah hangs the towel on a rack and uses a bathrobe instead.
  Visible consequence: The towel, hanging, drips continuously onto the bathrobe, soaking it.
  New information: The towel's discharge is constant and targets the driest nearby fabric.
Beat 3
  Action: Noah wraps a dry hand towel around his head.
  Visible consequence: The hand towel soaks up water from his hair and then squirts it onto the mirror, fogging it.
  New information: Towels transfer water to mirrors and glass, not just skin.
Beat 4
  Action: Noah uses a hairdryer to dry himself without a towel.
  Visible consequence: The hanging towel leaps off the rack and wraps around Noah's leg, transferring moisture back onto his skin.
  New information: The towel actively seeks the driest body part to wet.
Beat 5
  Action: Noah runs out of the bathroom naked, hoping to air dry.
  Visible consequence: The towel follows him, wrapping around his waist, and immediately releases a bucket of water onto the living room carpet.
  New information: The towel is attached to Noah's body and will never let him be dry.
finalPayoff: The towel follows him, wrapping around his waist, and immediately releases a bucket of water onto the living room carpet.
suggestedPayoffFamily: object attachment / goal never reached
loopPotential: Any attempt to get dry with a towel simply relocates the wetness, often back to Noah.
environment: Bathroom and adjacent living room
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Towel's absorbency reversed into discharge is clearly wrong; the chase adds physical comedy.

13. raw-013 — Tangling Hairbrush
Disposition: REVISION_REQUIRED | Eligibility: INELIGIBLE | SFE: 31 | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-013
title: Tangling Hairbrush
protagonistSuggestion: Opa
everydayGoal: Brush hair smooth
primaryObject: Hairbrush
normalExpectation: Brushing untangles hair and smooths it.
impossibleRule: Each brush stroke creates a new knot, and the knots tighten the more you brush; hair eventually coils around the brush like a topiary.
visibleTrigger: Opa brushes once, hair stands up in a giant knot.
whyThisObject: Brush bristles are meant to separate strands; if they weave strands together, brushing becomes tangling.
goalRuleRelationship: The action of brushing makes hair worse, so smooth hair is impossible.
frameZeroDescription: Opa stands in front of mirror holding a brush, but his hair is a twisted bird's nest with the brush embedded in it.
Beat 1
  Action: Opa pulls the brush out slowly, hoping to unwind the knot.
  Visible consequence: The knot tightens into a bun that pulls his head back.
  New information: Pulling the brush out causes the hair to cinch, not loosen.
Beat 2
  Action: Opa uses a wide-tooth comb to gently work through the tangle.
  Visible consequence: The comb adds more knots, and his hair expands into a giant sphere around his head.
  New information: Any grooming tool multiplies knots instead of removing them.
Beat 3
  Action: Opa applies hair conditioner to make strands slippery.
  Visible consequence: The hair becomes like rubber, and the brush bounces off, but the knots stretch and snap back with more loops.
  New information: Conditioner turns hair elastic, making tangles spring-loaded.
Beat 4
  Action: Opa puts on a hat to hide the hair and stop brushing.
  Visible consequence: The hat is sucked into the hair-knot and swallowed, disappearing into the tangled mass.
  New information: The hair's tangling now acts like a living trap, consuming objects.
Beat 5
  Action: Opa uses scissors to cut the hair away.
  Visible consequence: The hair regrows instantly into a larger, tighter knot, trapping the scissors.
  New information: Cutting triggers accelerated regrowth with more tangles.
finalPayoff: The hair regrows instantly into a larger, tighter knot, trapping the scissors.
suggestedPayoffFamily: logical extreme / effort worsens
loopPotential: Opa can try different tools, but the hair always re-tangles and often captures the tool.
environment: Bathroom with mirror
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Brush's detangling function reversed into knot-creation is visually clear; regrowth adds escalation.

14. raw-014 — Self-Healing Paper
Disposition: MALFORMED | Eligibility: NOT_EVALUATED | SFE: None | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-014
title: Self-Healing Paper
protagonistSuggestion: Kiko
everydayGoal: Cut paper for craft
primaryObject: Scissors
normalExpectation: Scissors shear paper into two pieces.
impossibleRule: As scissors cut, the paper immediately heals behind the blades, leaving no cut; the scissors themselves become slightly more blunt.
visibleTrigger: Kiko snips a paper, the cut line closes seamlessly.
whyThisObject: Scissors are meant to divide material; if the material self-heals, the cutting action is futile.
goalRuleRelationship: The intended cut never persists, so crafting cannot proceed.
frameZeroDescription: Kiko holds scissors mid-snip on a paper; the cut line behind the blades is sealing itself closed, with no separation visible.
Beat 1
  Action: Kiko cuts faster, moving scissors along a straight line.
  Visible consequence: The paper zips back together instantly, and the scissors make a zipper-like sound.
  New information: Speed of cutting increases the healing speed equally.
Beat 2
  Action: Kiko folds the paper and cuts through multiple layers.
  Visible consequence: The layers fuse together at the cut line, creating a thicker uncut stack.
  New information: Cutting multiple layers causes them to weld, not separate.
Beat 3
  Action: Kiko uses a craft knife instead of scissors.
  Visible consequence: The knife blade passes through but the paper instantly reattaches, and the knife's edge dulls to a butter knife shape.
  New information: The self-healing effect applies to any cutting tool and dulls the blade.
Beat 4
  Action: Kiko tries to tear the paper by hand.
  Visible consequence: The paper stretches like rubber and bounces back, flipping out of his hands.
  New information: The paper has become elastic, so it cannot be cut or torn.
Beat 5
  Action: Kiko gives up and tries to use pre-cut paper shapes from a packet.
  Visible consequence: The pre-cut shapes snap back into full sheets, and the scissors in his hand leap to the paper and start 'cutting' but leaving no marks.
  New information: The rule reverses relationship: tools cuts but material refuses to remain divided; even pre-existing cuts are undone.
finalPayoff: The pre-cut shapes snap back into full sheets, and the scissors in his hand leap to the paper and start 'cutting' but leaving no marks.
suggestedPayoffFamily: false assumption / effort negated
loopPotential: Any future cutting attempt heals; Kiko might try water or fire, but risk of new escalation.
environment: Craft table
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Scissors' dividing function is negated by self-healing paper; intuitive because cut line zips shut.

15. raw-015 — Bottom-Opening Zipper
Disposition: REJECTED | Eligibility: INELIGIBLE | SFE: 18 | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-015
title: Bottom-Opening Zipper
protagonistSuggestion: Mimi
everydayGoal: Zip up jacket to stay warm
primaryObject: Jacket zipper
normalExpectation: Pulling zipper up closes the jacket.
impossibleRule: As you pull the zipper tab upward, the bottom of the zipper separates, so the jacket opens from bottom while top closes; when you pull down, the bottom closes but top opens.
visibleTrigger: Mimi zips up to neck; the jacket's bottom half splits open, exposing her stomach.
whyThisObject: Zipper is designed to interlock teeth sequentially; if it opens opposite end, the closure is incomplete.
goalRuleRelationship: The action to close the jacket creates an opening elsewhere, so warmth cannot be retained.
frameZeroDescription: Mimi holds zipper tab at neck, jacket top is closed, but the bottom half hangs open, cold air whooshing through.
Beat 1
  Action: Mimi pulls zipper down to close the bottom gap.
  Visible consequence: The bottom zips shut but the top splits down to her chest.
  New information: The zipper has a pivot point; one end opens as the other closes.
Beat 2
  Action: Mimi holds the bottom together with one hand while zipping up with the other.
  Visible consequence: The zipper teeth at her hand pop apart, and her fingers get caught between them.
  New information: Physical holding doesn't prevent separation; the zipper actively parts around any obstruction.
Beat 3
  Action: Mimi zips halfway, hoping the middle stays closed.
  Visible consequence: Both top and bottom open outward like a mouth, and the jacket flaps open completely.
  New information: Halfway position opens both ends simultaneously.
Beat 4
  Action: Mimi turns the jacket inside out and zips from the other side.
  Visible consequence: The zipper now unzips from the middle, splitting the jacket into two separate halves that fall to the floor.
  New information: The zipper's failure mode changes with orientation, now bisects the garment.
Beat 5
  Action: Mimi grabs a safety pin to pin the bottom closed.
  Visible consequence: The safety pin zips itself into the zipper track and the entire jacket rolls up like a window blind, squeezing Mimi's torso.
  New information: The zipper treats any inserted object as a pull tab and activates full closure upward, trapping Mimi.
finalPayoff: The safety pin zips itself into the zipper track and the entire jacket rolls up like a window blind, squeezing Mimi's torso.
suggestedPayoffFamily: unexpected causal chain / self-trapping
loopPotential: Further attempts to unfasten may cause the jacket to roll or unroll unpredictably.
environment: Cold windy street
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Zipper's interlocking action is spatially inverted, so closing one end opens the other; visually clear and blocks warmth.

Disposition: REVISION_REQUIRED | Eligibility: INELIGIBLE | SFE: 36 | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-016
title: Bottomless Backpack
protagonistSuggestion: Arda
everydayGoal: Pack backpack for school
primaryObject: Backpack
normalExpectation: Items placed in top stay inside until you open it.
impossibleRule: The bottom of the backpack opens whenever it is upright; when turned upside down, the bottom seals but the top opens, so contents always spill.
visibleTrigger: Arda puts books in upright backpack; they fall through bottom onto floor.
whyThisObject: Backpack is a container; if gravity works against the orientation, it cannot hold items.
goalRuleRelationship: The act of placing items inside directly causes them to exit through bottom, preventing packing.
frameZeroDescription: Arda holds a backpack upright with several books halfway falling through the open bottom, while the top zipper is closed.
Beat 1
  Action: Arda turns the backpack upside down to use the sealed bottom as top.
  Visible consequence: The original top zipper opens, and books pour out onto his head.
  New information: The pack has two openings, and gravity always finds the open one.
Beat 2
  Action: Arda holds the backpack sideways and loads books through the side.
  Visible consequence: A new side seam splits open, and all contents slide out horizontally.
  New information: Any orientation creates a new opening at the lowest point.
Beat 3
  Action: Arda stuffs clothes into the bottom to block it, then adds books.
  Visible consequence: The clothes squeeze through the bottom seam, pulling the books with them, leaving an empty flattened pack.
  New information: The bottom opening acts like a drain that vacuums contents out.
Beat 4
  Action: Arda puts the backpack on his back, hoping body pressure seals the bottom.
  Visible consequence: The pack inflates and launches off his shoulders, spinning in midair as contents rainbow out.
  New information: Wearing the pack triggers ejection, not sealing.
Beat 5
  Action: Arda gives up and carries books in his arms.
  Visible consequence: The backpack slides over and swallows the books from his arms, then spits them out the bottom into a neat pile at his feet.
  New information: The pack wants to be filled but immediately empties itself as soon as it contains anything.
finalPayoff: The backpack slides over and swallows the books from his arms, then spits them out the bottom into a neat pile at his feet.
suggestedPayoffFamily: impossible containment / object agency
loopPotential: Any future loading attempt triggers the same drain; Arda might need a bottomless bag that is actually bottomless.
environment: Bedroom floor
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Backpack's containment is inverted by gravity-dependent openings; the drain-like bottom is visually clear.

17. raw-017 — Self-Tucking Bed Sheets
Disposition: MALFORMED | Eligibility: NOT_EVALUATED | SFE: None | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-017
title: Self-Tucking Bed Sheets
protagonistSuggestion: Luca
everydayGoal: Get out of bed in the morning
primaryObject: Bed sheet
normalExpectation: You push sheets off and stand up.
impossibleRule: The more you push sheets away, the tighter they tuck around your body; sheets re-tuck themselves under the mattress as you move.
visibleTrigger: Luca throws off blanket; it snaps back over him.
whyThisObject: Bedsheets are meant to cover; if they actively trap, getting up becomes a wrestling match.
goalRuleRelationship: The action to remove sheets causes them to rewrap, preventing standing.
frameZeroDescription: Luca lies in bed with one leg out, but the sheet has wrapped around his leg and is pulling him back toward the mattress.
Beat 1
  Action: Luca sits up and pushes the sheet to the foot of the bed.
  Visible consequence: The sheet springs back and tucks itself under his chin, pinning his arms to his sides.
  New information: The sheet's fabric is elastic and self-tucking.
Beat 2
  Action: Luca rolls toward the edge to slide out.
  Visible consequence: The sheet rolls with him, winding around his torso like a burrito.
  New information: Moving doesn't loosen; it increases winding.
Beat 3
  Action: Luca kicks violently to break the wrap.
  Visible consequence: The sheet stretches into ropes that tie his ankles to the bed frame.
  New information: The sheet can transform into adjustable restraints, comedic but not harmful.
Beat 4
  Action: Luca tries to crawl out from the bottom of the bed.
  Visible consequence: The fitted sheet pops off the mattress and becomes a parachute, dragging him back to the center.
  New information: The whole sheet detaches and acts as a net.
Beat 5
  Action: Luca calls for help, and Mimi enters to pull him out.
  Visible consequence: The sheet wraps around both of them, tucking them together like a mummy, while the bed bounces.
  New information: The sheet's tucking extends to anyone who tries to intervene.
finalPayoff: The sheet wraps around both of them, tucking them together like a mummy, while the bed bounces.
suggestedPayoffFamily: goal blocked by self-tucking / role reversal
loopPotential: Any morning, Luca will be held by sheets until the bed decides to release him.
environment: Bedroom
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Bedsheets' covering function becomes trapping; intuitive because throwing off sheets should free you, not rewrap you.

19. raw-019 — Microwave Hot Plate
Disposition: REJECTED | Eligibility: INELIGIBLE | SFE: 18 | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-019
title: Microwave Hot Plate
protagonistSuggestion: Opa
everydayGoal: Reheat leftovers
primaryObject: Microwave oven
normalExpectation: Microwave heats food evenly.
impossibleRule: The microwave heats the plate to scalding while food remains frozen solid; any attempt to remove plate transfers heat to food, flash-thawing it but also melting plate.
visibleTrigger: Opa opens microwave, plate glows red, food has ice crystals.
whyThisObject: Microwave's energy is meant to excite water in food; if it excites the plate material, food stays cold.
goalRuleRelationship: The action of heating food results in dangerous hot plate and cold food, preventing eating.
frameZeroDescription: Opa opens microwave, steam rises from the plate, but the food on it is visibly frozen with frost.
Beat 1
  Action: Opa uses oven mitts to remove the hot plate.
  Visible consequence: The mitts start smoking, and the plate's heat melts a hole through the mitt, dropping the plate onto the counter, which sizzles.
  New information: The plate is hotter than expected; it transfers heat outward.
Beat 2
  Action: Opa runs the plate under cold water to cool it.
  Visible consequence: The water flashes to steam, and the food thaws instantly but is now soggy and boiling.
  New information: Water contact transfers heat to food but also overcooks it.
Beat 3
  Action: Opa puts the frozen food on a different plate and microwaves again.
  Visible consequence: The new plate heats red, food remains frozen, and the microwave's glass window fogs.
  New information: The microwave always targets the plate material, not food.
Beat 4
  Action: Opa tries to microwave the food without a plate, directly on the turntable.
  Visible consequence: The microwave refuses to start, beeping and displaying 'PLATE REQUIRED' in glowing letters.
  New information: The appliance demands a plate to activate, ensuring impossible heating.
Beat 5
  Action: Opa gives up and eats the frozen food directly.
  Visible consequence: The hot plate jumps from counter onto Opa's lap, thawing the food on his fork instantly but also scorching his pants.
  New information: The plate can transfer its heat to the food when close, but always at the cost of burning surroundings.
finalPayoff: The hot plate jumps from counter onto Opa's lap, thawing the food on his fork instantly but also scorching his pants.
suggestedPayoffFamily: heat transfer inversion / self-created hazard
loopPotential: Any reheat attempt repeats the plate heating; Opa might use a metal plate, causing sparks (child-safe comedic but not shown?).
environment: Kitchen
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Microwave's selective heating is subverted; visually clear with ice on food and glowing plate.

20. raw-020 — Wrong-Room Light Switch
Disposition: REVISION_REQUIRED | Eligibility: INELIGIBLE | SFE: 32 | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-020
title: Wrong-Room Light Switch
protagonistSuggestion: Kiko
everydayGoal: Turn on living room light
primaryObject: Light switch
normalExpectation: Flipping switch controls nearest room's light.
impossibleRule: The switch controls the light in a different room (e.g., bathroom), while the room you're in stays dark; each switch in house controls a random other room.
visibleTrigger: Kiko flips living room switch; hall light turns on, living room dark.
whyThisObject: Light switches are spatially tied to their room; cross-wiring makes them useless for local control.
goalRuleRelationship: The action to illuminate your room instead lights a distant room, leaving you in dark.
frameZeroDescription: Kiko stands in dark living room flipping a switch; through the doorway, the bathroom light is on, and the living room remains dark.
Beat 1
  Action: Kiko flips the switch repeatedly, hoping to get the right room.
  Visible consequence: Various lights in the house turn on and off in a pattern, but the living room stays dark while the porch light blinks.
  New information: The switch cycles through all other rooms except the one he's in.
Beat 2
  Action: Kiko walks to the bathroom to flip that switch.
  Visible consequence: The bathroom light turns off, and the living room light turns on.
  New information: Switches are paired with opposite rooms; you must be in the room you don't want lit.
Beat 3
  Action: Kiko stands in the doorway between rooms to flip both switches.
  Visible consequence: Both rooms go dark, and a flickering light appears in the attic.
  New information: Simultaneous toggles transfer power to an unswitched room.
Beat 4
  Action: Kiko uses a flashlight to see while navigating the switch panel.
  Visible consequence: The flashlight turns on, but its beam illuminates the wrong room through a window, and Kiko stumbles.
  New information: The cross-wiring extends to all light sources, including portable ones.
Beat 5
  Action: Kiko gives up and uses candles.
  Visible consequence: The candles' flames cast shadows that point to a hidden switch behind a bookshelf; flipping that switch turns on the sun outside, causing full daylight indoors.
  New information: There is a master switch that controls daylight, not indoor lights, solving the darkness but at absurd scale.
finalPayoff: The candles' flames cast shadows that point to a hidden switch behind a bookshelf; flipping that switch turns on the sun outside, causing full daylight indoors.
suggestedPayoffFamily: unexpected causal success via wrong switch
loopPotential: The daylight switch may reset at night, forcing Kiko to find another hidden control.
environment: House interior, multiple rooms
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Light switch's local function is scrambled; intuitive because you see wrong room light up.

21. raw-021 — Upward Faucet
Disposition: MALFORMED | Eligibility: NOT_EVALUATED | SFE: None | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-021
title: Upward Faucet
protagonistSuggestion: Mimi
everydayGoal: Fill a glass with water
primaryObject: Faucet
normalExpectation: Water flows downward out of spout into glass.
impossibleRule: Water flows upward from the faucet to the ceiling when turned on; turning off causes water to rain down only in the sink area.
visibleTrigger: Mimi turns on faucet; water arcs up to ceiling like a fountain.
whyThisObject: Faucet relies on gravity to pour water; reversing flow direction makes collection impossible.
goalRuleRelationship: The action to fill glass causes water to go the wrong way, so glass never fills.
frameZeroDescription: Mimi holds a glass under a faucet, but a vertical stream of water shoots upward to the ceiling, and none falls into the glass.
Beat 1
  Action: Mimi holds the glass above the faucet, upside down, to catch the upward stream.
  Visible consequence: The water flows into the glass but immediately shoots out the top like a second fountain, emptying it.
  New information: The water's upward momentum continues through any container.
Beat 2
  Action: Mimi turns the faucet off and collects the falling rainwater that comes down.
  Visible consequence: The rain falls directly around the sink but not into the glass, and the glass itself floats upward.
  New information: When faucet is off, water falls, but the glass becomes buoyant and rises away.
Beat 3
  Action: Mimi puts a lid on the glass and stands on a chair to catch water at ceiling level.
  Visible consequence: The water pressure pushes the lid off, and the glass sticks to the ceiling, filling with water but inaccessible.
  New information: The ceiling acts as a reservoir; water gathers there and traps containers.
Beat 4
  Action: Mimi uses a bucket and mop to sweep water from ceiling down.
  Visible consequence: The bucket also floats up and sticks to ceiling, splashing water everywhere but down.
  New information: Objects near the upward flow become anti-gravity and collect on ceiling.
Beat 5
  Action: Mimi calls Luca to help, and he turns the faucet valve under the sink.
  Visible consequence: The water stops, and all the ceiling water falls at once, filling the room waist-deep and floating the glass to Mimi.
  New information: Shutting off the valve releases the accumulated water all at once, causing a flood but delivering the glass.
finalPayoff: The water stops, and all the ceiling water falls at once, filling the room waist-deep and floating the glass to Mimi.
suggestedPayoffFamily: delayed consequence / flood payoff
loopPotential: Any future faucet use causes ceiling reservoir and eventual indoor rain.
environment: Kitchen
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Faucet's gravity-driven flow reversed into upward fountain; direct block to filling glass.

22. raw-022 — Collapsing Chair
Disposition: REVISION_REQUIRED | Eligibility: INELIGIBLE | SFE: 25 | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-022
title: Collapsing Chair
protagonistSuggestion: Arda
everydayGoal: Sit down at desk
primaryObject: Chair
normalExpectation: Chair supports weight when sat on.
impossibleRule: The chair remains rigid when empty, but the instant any weight is applied, its legs fold flat, dropping occupant to floor; when empty, it pops back upright.
visibleTrigger: Arda sits, chair flattens beneath him.
whyThisObject: Chair's structural function is support; conditional collapse makes sitting impossible.
goalRuleRelationship: The action of sitting triggers the loss of support, so you can never rest in chair.
frameZeroDescription: Arda is mid-air just above a chair that has collapsed flat on the floor, legs splayed outward.
Beat 1
  Action: Arda checks the chair, stands it up, and presses on the seat with one hand.
  Visible consequence: The chair collapses instantly under the light hand pressure, then springs back.
  New information: Even partial weight triggers collapse; no weight means rigid.
Beat 2
  Action: Arda tries to sit very slowly, easing weight onto the seat.
  Visible consequence: The chair collapses progressively, lowering him to the floor in a controlled slide, then snaps shut on his fingers.
  New information: Slow approach doesn't prevent collapse; it just prolongs the drop.
Beat 3
  Action: Arda puts a stack of books on the chair to hold it steady.
  Visible consequence: The chair stays upright, but when Arda sits on the books, the chair collapses sideways and books scatter.
  New information: Distributed weight still triggers collapse as long as it includes Arda.
Beat 4
  Action: Arda stands on the chair instead of sitting.
  Visible consequence: The chair flings him forward like a catapult, launching him onto the desk.
  New information: The chair's collapse mechanism can store and release energy when weight is applied.
Beat 5
  Action: Arda gives up and sits on the floor.
  Visible consequence: The chair, now empty, creeps over and positions itself under him, then collapses flat, leaving him sitting on a folded chair.
  New information: The chair is attracted to being sat on but cannot support, so it ends up as a flattened seat.
finalPayoff: The chair, now empty, creeps over and positions itself under him, then collapses flat, leaving him sitting on a folded chair.
suggestedPayoffFamily: object agency / goal partially achieved absurdly
loopPotential: Arda might use a different chair, but the same collapse rule may apply to its twin.
environment: Home office
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Chair's support function is conditionally removed; visible collapse on sitting is classic but we vary with self-moving chair.

23. raw-023 — Accelerating Ball
Disposition: MALFORMED | Eligibility: NOT_EVALUATED | SFE: None | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-023
title: Accelerating Ball
protagonistSuggestion: Luca
everydayGoal: Play catch with Kiko
primaryObject: Ball
normalExpectation: Thrown ball travels to friend and can be caught.
impossibleRule: Any throw causes the ball to accelerate away from the thrower, gaining speed with distance, until it orbits back and hits nothing.
visibleTrigger: Luca throws gently; ball shoots off and disappears over horizon, then returns like a rocket.
whyThisObject: Ball's purpose is to be thrown and caught; if it accelerates uncontrollably, catch is impossible.
goalRuleRelationship: The throwing action intended to send ball to friend instead accelerates it away, preventing catch.
frameZeroDescription: Luca's arm is extended in a throwing motion, but the ball is already a blur leaving a trail, while Kiko stands empty-handed far away.
Beat 1
  Action: Luca throws the ball as softly as possible.
  Visible consequence: The ball still zooms off, curving around a tree and smashing into a fence, then bounces back at high speed.
  New information: The ball's acceleration is independent of throw force.
Beat 2
  Action: Kiko runs to intercept the returning ball.
  Visible consequence: The ball swerves around Kiko, gaining more speed, and orbits the yard in a loop.
  New information: The ball actively avoids being caught.
Beat 3
  Action: Luca and Kiko stand back-to-back and each holds a glove to trap the ball.
  Visible consequence: The ball bounces between their gloves, never being caught, and knocks them both over.
  New information: The ball treats gloves as new launch pads, increasing chaos.
Beat 4
  Action: Luca tries to trap the ball with a bucket.
  Visible consequence: The ball ricochets off the bucket's rim, accelerates to supersonic speed, and creates a sonic boom.
  New information: The ball's speed now breaks sound barrier, making it uncatchable and noisy.
Beat 5
  Action: Both give up and walk away.
  Visible consequence: The ball, losing speed, lands gently in Kiko's open hand, then falls asleep with a snore.
  New information: The ball only becomes catchable when no one is trying to catch it.
finalPayoff: The ball, losing speed, lands gently in Kiko's open hand, then falls asleep with a snore.
suggestedPayoffFamily: goal reached wrongly after abandon
loopPotential: Any new attempt to play catch restarts the acceleration; the ball only cooperates when ignored.
environment: Backyard
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Ball's projectile motion is exaggerated into self-acceleration; intuitive and blocks catch.

B. NORMALIZED / DETERMINISTIC INTERPRETATION

24. raw-024 — Book Jumps to End
Disposition: REVISION_REQUIRED | Eligibility: INELIGIBLE | SFE: 35 | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-024
title: Book Jumps to End
protagonistSuggestion: Noah
everydayGoal: Read the first chapter
primaryObject: Book
normalExpectation: Opening book at page 1 shows beginning.
impossibleRule: Whenever you try to read from the beginning, the pages turn themselves rapidly to the last page; the text also rearranges so the story reads backward from end to start.
visibleTrigger: Noah opens book at page 1; pages flip to end, and the first words are 'The End.'
whyThisObject: Book is meant to be read sequentially; if it forces you to the end, reading the start is impossible.
goalRuleRelationship: The action of reading from page 1 triggers skipping to end, preventing normal progress.
frameZeroDescription: Noah holds a book open, but the pages are a blur, and the visible page shows the final chapter with 'The End' at the bottom.
Beat 1
  Action: Noah holds the first page down with his thumb and tries to read.
  Visible consequence: The pages underneath ripple and flip, pushing his thumb out and snapping the book shut.
  New information: The book can generate internal wind to turn pages.
Beat 2
  Action: Noah starts reading from the end, hoping the reversed story still makes sense.
  Visible consequence: The text on the last page reads normally, but when he turns back one page, the words are upside down.
  New information: Reading backward causes alternating page orientations.
Beat 3
  Action: Noah uses a bookmark to keep the page, but the bookmark is ejected like a projectile.
  Visible consequence: The book opens to a random middle page, and the story is now a recipe for soup.
  New information: The book's content changes based on which page is forced open.
Beat 4
  Action: Noah photocopies page 1 and reads the copy.
  Visible consequence: The copy's text disappears, and the book's page 1 becomes blank.
  New information: The book can transfer its text to any copy and then erase it.
Beat 5
  Action: Noah closes the book and listens to an audiobook instead.
  Visible consequence: The audiobook begins with the final chapter, and the physical book flips pages in sync with the audio, ending in 3 seconds.
  New information: The impossible rule extends to all versions of the story.
finalPayoff: The audiobook begins with the final chapter, and the physical book flips pages in sync with the audio, ending in 3 seconds.
suggestedPayoffFamily: function transferred to alternative medium
loopPotential: Any attempt to experience the story from the beginning will be skipped to the end.
environment: Living room armchair
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Book's sequential reading is inverted by auto-flipping to end; visually clear with page blur.

25. raw-025 — Self-Knotting Laces
Disposition: REVISION_REQUIRED | Eligibility: INELIGIBLE | SFE: 31 | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-025
title: Self-Knotting Laces
protagonistSuggestion: Opa
everydayGoal: Tie shoes before walk
primaryObject: Shoe laces
normalExpectation: Laces tie into a bow that stays.
impossibleRule: As you tie a bow, the laces weave themselves into additional knots, becoming tighter and more complex with every pull; untying creates two new knots.
visibleTrigger: Opa ties a simple bow; the laces instantly form a cloverleaf knot.
whyThisObject: Laces are meant to secure shoe with a single bow; self-knotting prevents both tying and untying.
goalRuleRelationship: The action of tying laces overcomplicates them, making shoes impossible to put on or remove.
frameZeroDescription: Opa sits on a bench with one shoe on, but the laces are a mass of tangled knots covering the shoe.
Beat 1
  Action: Opa pulls one end to untangle the knot.
  Visible consequence: The laces form a figure-eight around his ankle, tightening the shoe.
  New information: Pulling any lace end adds a new knot and tightens.
Beat 2
  Action: Opa uses a fork to pick apart the knots.
  Visible consequence: The fork gets woven into the laces, and the shoe jumps off his foot, still knotted with fork.
  New information: Tools get incorporated into the self-knotting laces.
Beat 3
  Action: Opa removes the shoe and works on it in his lap.
  Visible consequence: The laces knot around his fingers, tying his hands to the shoe.
  New information: The laces can target body parts as new knots.
Beat 4
  Action: Opa puts the shoe under running water to loosen fibers.
  Visible consequence: The wet laces shrink and knot tighter, turning the shoe into a hard ball.
  New information: Water worsens the knots due to shrinking.
Beat 5
  Action: Opa gives up and walks barefoot.
  Visible consequence: The knotted shoe follows him, hopping along, and the laces untie themselves into a perfect bow on the other shoe.
  New information: The laces only cooperate when no one is touching them, and they switch shoes.
finalPayoff: The knotted shoe follows him, hopping along, and the laces untie themselves into a perfect bow on the other shoe.
suggestedPayoffFamily: role reversal / object self-resolves when ignored
loopPotential: Opa might try to tie the good shoe, which could start the knotting again.
environment: Park bench
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Laces' tying function is exaggerated into over-knotting; immediate block to wearing shoes.

26. raw-026 — Bending Nail Hammer
Disposition: REJECTED | Eligibility: INELIGIBLE | SFE: 19 | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-026
title: Bending Nail Hammer
protagonistSuggestion: Kiko
everydayGoal: Hang a picture frame
primaryObject: Hammer
normalExpectation: Hammering a nail drives it straight into wall.
impossibleRule: Every hammer strike causes the nail to bend away from the wall at a right angle; bending increases with force.
visibleTrigger: Kiko hits nail; it curves 90 degrees without entering wall.
whyThisObject: Hammer is meant to apply axial force to nail; if nail deflects under any force, it cannot penetrate.
goalRuleRelationship: The action to hang frame fails as nail never goes straight, preventing secure mounting.
frameZeroDescription: Kiko holds hammer mid-swing, but the nail protruding from the wall is bent into a U-shape, and the picture frame leans beside him.
Beat 1
  Action: Kiko holds the nail straight with pliers and hammers gently.
  Visible consequence: The nail bends around the pliers and grips them, trapping the tool.
  New information: The nail's bending is directional and can ensnare nearby tools.
Beat 2
  Action: Kiko uses a longer nail, hoping it will go straight.
  Visible consequence: The long nail curls into a spiral, then springs back and pings off the wall.
  New information: Nail length increases elasticity, causing projectile bending.
Beat 3
  Action: Kiko pre-drills a hole, then taps the nail.
  Visible consequence: The nail passes halfway, then bends sideways and splits the wallboard, making a large hole.
  New information: The nail bends at a specific depth, damaging the wall.
Beat 4
  Action: Kiko gives up on nails and uses adhesive strips.
  Visible consequence: The hammer leaps from his hand, straightens the bent nail with one tap, then hammers it perfectly into the wall.
  New information: The hammer can install nails only when not held by Kiko.
Beat 5
  Action: Kiko grabs the hammer to adjust the picture, but the hammer now bends every nail in the house.
  Visible consequence: All nails in the room curve, pictures crash, and the original nail pings into Kiko's hand, bending his thumb comically.
  New information: The hammer's bent-nail effect spreads to all previously straight nails.
finalPayoff: All nails in the room curve, pictures crash, and the original nail pings into Kiko's hand, bending his thumb comically.
suggestedPayoffFamily: object agency / effort causes wider damage
loopPotential: Any future hanging requires avoiding hammers, but the hammer may seek Kiko out.
environment: Living room wall
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Hammer's force is supposed to drive nail; nail bending under that force is visually obvious and blocks hanging.

Disposition: REVISION_REQUIRED | Eligibility: INELIGIBLE | SFE: 33 | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-027
title: Opposite-Color Paintbrush
protagonistSuggestion: Mimi
everydayGoal: Paint wall blue
primaryObject: Paintbrush
normalExpectation: Brush applies paint color loaded on it.
impossibleRule: Whatever color you load, the brush deposits the complementary color; loading blue paints orange, loading red paints green.
visibleTrigger: Mimi strokes blue paint; wall turns orange.
whyThisObject: Brush is a transfer tool for pigment; if it inverts color, painting a chosen color is impossible.
goalRuleRelationship: The action to apply blue paint produces orange, so the wall never becomes blue.
frameZeroDescription: Mimi stands at a wall with a blue paint bucket and brush mid-stroke; the painted patch is vivid orange.
Beat 1
  Action: Mimi mixes a custom color equal parts blue and orange, hoping inversion cancels.
  Visible consequence: The brush paints a shimmering gray that immediately splits into blue and orange stripes.
  New information: Complementary inversion doesn't cancel but separates into two colors.
Beat 2
  Action: Mimi tries to paint the wall orange, expecting inversion to give blue.
  Visible consequence: The wall turns blue-green, not pure blue, and the brush swirls automatically.
  New information: The inversion rule includes a hue shift, so aiming for orange doesn't yield blue.
Beat 3
  Action: Mimi uses a roller instead of brush.
  Visible consequence: The roller paints the opposite color at triple scale, covering the wall in a giant orange sunburst.
  New information: The inversion applies to any painting tool, increasing area.
Beat 4
  Action: Mimi puts a stencil on the wall to paint a pattern.
  Visible consequence: The stencil areas stay original color, while the exposed wall turns complementary, creating a negative of the pattern.
  New information: The brush inverts color in the open areas, not just strokes.
Beat 5
  Action: Mimi gives up and paints the wall back white.
  Visible consequence: The white paint turns black, then the entire wall flashes and becomes a mirror reflecting the room in inverted colors.
  New information: Painting white triggers a full-wall color inversion, turning the wall into a funhouse mirror.
finalPayoff: The white paint turns black, then the entire wall flashes and becomes a mirror reflecting the room in inverted colors.
suggestedPayoffFamily: logical extreme / environmental transformation
loopPotential: Any new paint attempt may cause new color chaos; the wall can never be one color.
environment: Room being painted
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Paintbrush's pigment transfer is inverted; visually clear when blue becomes orange.

28. raw-028 — Opposite Autocorrect
Disposition: MALFORMED | Eligibility: NOT_EVALUATED | SFE: None | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-028
title: Opposite Autocorrect
protagonistSuggestion: Luca
everydayGoal: Text friend to meet at 5
primaryObject: Phone
normalExpectation: Typing words sends intended message.
impossibleRule: The phone autocorrects every word to its opposite meaning (e.g., 'yes' becomes 'no', '5' becomes 'five thousand'), and sends instantly before you can edit.
visibleTrigger: Luca types 'Meet at 5' and message sends as 'Melt at five thousand'.
whyThisObject: Phone's autocorrect is supposed to help, but if it inverts meaning, communication is reversed.
goalRuleRelationship: The action of texting the plan sends the opposite plan, so friend goes to wrong place/time.
frameZeroDescription: Luca looks at phone screen; the sent message reads 'Melt at five thousand' while the intended message is visible above as typed but crossed out.
Beat 1
  Action: Luca types double negatives to get positive meaning.
  Visible consequence: The phone autocorrects to opposite of double negative, resulting in confusing triple negative and message sends 'I won't not never meet'.
  New information: Inversion is literal but compounds with grammar.
Beat 2
  Action: Luca uses emojis instead of words.
  Visible consequence: Emojis flip to their opposites: thumbs up becomes thumbs down, smiley becomes angry.
  New information: The autocorrect applies to all symbols, not just text.
Beat 3
  Action: Luca calls instead of texting.
  Visible consequence: His voice comes out reversed, saying 'Meet at five' as 'Evif ta teem', and the friend hears gibberish.
  New information: The phone's inversion extends to audio, reversing speech.
Beat 4
  Action: Luca uses a different phone, borrowing Mimi's.
  Visible consequence: Mimi's phone also autocorrects, sending 'Don't meet' but then the original phone sends the correct text spontaneously.
  New information: The rule follows Luca, not the device; only the first phone can sometimes correct itself.
Beat 5
  Action: Luca tries to edit the sent message by appending 'ignore previous', but that also inverts.
  Visible consequence: The friend receives a series of contradictory messages and finally shows up at 5 AM, throwing a pillow at Luca.
  New information: The accumulation of opposite texts leads to the friend arriving at opposite time.
finalPayoff: The friend receives a series of contradictory messages and finally shows up at 5 AM, throwing a pillow at Luca.
suggestedPayoffFamily: delayed consequence / communication inversion
loopPotential: Any future text will be reversed, forcing Luca to speak in person or use smoke signals.
environment: Living room, phone in hand
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Phone autocorrect is meant to fix errors; reversing meaning creates direct goal interference.
