29. raw-029 — Dirtying Washing Machine
Disposition: REVISION_REQUIRED | Eligibility: INELIGIBLE | SFE: 32 | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-029
title: Dirtying Washing Machine
protagonistSuggestion: Arda
everydayGoal: Clean clothes
primaryObject: Washing machine
normalExpectation: Clothes come out cleaner.
impossibleRule: The machine extracts dirt from clothes and redeposits it onto another item in the same load, so the load comes out with one clean item and the rest soot-black.
visibleTrigger: Arda opens washer, one white shirt pristine, other clothes blackened.
whyThisObject: Washer is meant to remove dirt; if it just moves dirt between items, cleaning is incomplete.
goalRuleRelationship: The action to wash clothes leaves most dirtier, preventing clean laundry.
frameZeroDescription: Arda looks into open washing machine; a single clean white shirt sits atop a pile of blackened garments.
Beat 1
  Action: Arda separates whites and colors, running two loads.
  Visible consequence: In the white load, dirt migrates to the machine's drum, which turns black and rubs off on all whites.
  New information: The machine can hold and transfer dirt to its own walls.
Beat 2
  Action: Arda puts all clothes in with extra detergent.
  Visible consequence: The detergent bubbles turn gray, and the clothes come out tie-dyed with dirt patterns.
  New information: More detergent spreads dirt more evenly, creating stains.
Beat 3
  Action: Arda wipes the drum clean before running again.
  Visible consequence: The wiped drum stays clean, but the clothes now emerge with mud splatters exactly where fabric touched the drum.
  New information: The machine deposits dirt at contact points, so any cycle dirties.
Beat 4
  Action: Arda uses a mesh laundry bag to isolate each item.
  Visible consequence: Each bag becomes a dirt packet; one bag bursts, sprinkling dry dirt like a pepper shaker.
  New information: Isolating clothes turns each into a dirt bomb.
Beat 5
  Action: Arda gives up and takes clothes to a river to wash by hand.
  Visible consequence: The washing machine follows him on its spin cycle, rolling down the street, and the clothes he washed in the river absorb dirt from the machine's wake.
  New information: The machine pursues Arda and re-dirties any clothes he cleans elsewhere.
finalPayoff: The washing machine follows him on its spin cycle, rolling down the street, and the clothes he washed in the river absorb dirt from the machine's wake.
suggestedPayoffFamily: environmental takeover / object pursues
loopPotential: Any washing attempt, anywhere, attracts the machine's dirt.
environment: Laundry room and street
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Washer's cleaning function is reversed into dirt redistribution; visually obvious with one clean item among blackened.

30. raw-030 — Spiky Ice Cube Tray
Disposition: REVISION_REQUIRED | Eligibility: INELIGIBLE | SFE: 28 | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-030
title: Spiky Ice Cube Tray
protagonistSuggestion: Noah
everydayGoal: Make ice cubes for drinks
primaryObject: Ice cube tray
normalExpectation: Water in tray freezes into cube shapes.
impossibleRule: The water freezes into sharp upward spikes that puncture the plastic tray and any covering, growing toward the ceiling.
visibleTrigger: Noah opens freezer, ice spikes have pierced the tray and are growing out.
whyThisObject: Ice tray forms cubes via expansion; if freezing instead creates directional spikes, ice is unusable and sharp-looking.
goalRuleRelationship: The action to make cubes results in spiky ice that cannot be extracted safely, preventing cold drink.
frameZeroDescription: Noah opens freezer; a tray is pierced by icy spikes pointing upward like a hedgehog.
Beat 1
  Action: Noah tries to break off a spike by hand.
  Visible consequence: The spike snaps but instantly regrows longer and sharper, nicking the freezer shelf.
  New information: The ice regrows when broken, faster each time.
Beat 2
  Action: Noah runs warm water over the tray to melt spikes.
  Visible consequence: The spikes melt into a small flood that refreezes into an even larger spike cluster on the counter.
  New information: Melted water refreezes into spikes anywhere, not just tray.
Beat 3
  Action: Noah places the tray upside down in the freezer, hoping spikes grow downward.
  Visible consequence: The spikes grow upward relative to gravity, piercing through the tray bottom and sticking out the top.
  New information: The spike growth is gravity-independent, always along the vertical axis of the tray's original orientation.
Beat 4
  Action: Noah uses a different silicone tray to make cubes.
  Visible consequence: The silicone stretches around the spikes, forming a balloon that inflates until it pops, scattering ice shards.
  New information: Flexible trays can't contain the spikes; they stretch into a balloon.
Beat 5
  Action: Noah gives up and buys a bag of ice from the store.
  Visible consequence: The bag of ice starts sprouting spikes inside the plastic bag, and the bag floats up to the ceiling, freezing a chandelier.
  New information: The spike formation follows any purchased ice, spreading to the environment.
finalPayoff: The bag of ice starts sprouting spikes inside the plastic bag, and the bag floats up to the ceiling, freezing a chandelier.
suggestedPayoffFamily: environmental takeover / growth
loopPotential: Any future ice-making or ice-buying will produce spikes, so cold drinks become impossible.
environment: Kitchen and freezer
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Ice cube tray's freezing function is subverted into spike growth; visually dramatic and blocks use.

31. raw-031 — Shadow Flashlight
Disposition: REVISION_REQUIRED | Eligibility: INELIGIBLE | SFE: 25 | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-031
title: Shadow Flashlight
protagonistSuggestion: Opa
everydayGoal: Find keys in dark room
primaryObject: Flashlight
normalExpectation: Flashlight produces light beam to illuminate.
impossibleRule: The flashlight absorbs light and projects a darkness beam; pointing it at objects makes them disappear into shadow, while surroundings stay dim.
visibleTrigger: Opa turns on flashlight, a black cone shoots out, making the area darker.
whyThisObject: Flashlight's purpose is to emit light; emitting darkness is its inverse.
goalRuleRelationship: The action to illuminate keys instead casts them into deeper shadow, preventing search.
frameZeroDescription: Opa points a flashlight at the floor, but a black beam covers the area where keys should be, while the rest of the room is faint.
Beat 1
  Action: Opa shakes the flashlight and changes batteries.
  Visible consequence: The darkness beam becomes wider, swallowing a chair and half the room into blackness.
  New information: More power increases darkness, not light.
Beat 2
  Action: Opa points the flashlight at the ceiling, hoping reflected light reveals keys.
  Visible consequence: The ceiling turns black and starts dripping shadow like ink, covering the floor.
  New information: The darkness is a substance that can drip and spread.
Beat 3
  Action: Opa turns on a lamp instead.
  Visible consequence: The lamp works normally, but the flashlight's shadow beam seeks out the lamp and extinguishes it.
  New information: The darkness beam actively cancels any other light source.
Beat 4
  Action: Opa uses the flashlight in reverse, pointing it at his own eyes.
  Visible consequence: Instead of darkness, a bright beam shines out the back of the flashlight, illuminating a corner where keys are visible.
  New information: The flashlight has reversed polarity: front emits dark, back emits light.
Beat 5
  Action: Opa picks up the keys using the light from the back.
  Visible consequence: The keys glow and float into the flashlight's front beam, where they vanish into shadow, never to be found.
  New information: The flashlight's front beam can erase objects, not just darken them.
finalPayoff: The keys glow and float into the flashlight's front beam, where they vanish into shadow, never to be found.
suggestedPayoffFamily: absorption / property transfer
loopPotential: The keys might reappear if Opa uses the back beam, but then the front beam may erase them again.
environment: Dark hallway
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Flashlight's light emission is inverted to shadow projection; intuitive and blocks finding keys.

32. raw-032 — Delayed Mirror
Disposition: REVISION_REQUIRED | Eligibility: INELIGIBLE | SFE: 33 | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-032
title: Delayed Mirror
protagonistSuggestion: Kiko
everydayGoal: Fix hair using mirror
primaryObject: Wall mirror
normalExpectation: Mirror shows real-time reflection.
impossibleRule: The mirror shows a reflection delayed by one second, so Kiko sees her previous movement; when she moves to adjust, the lag causes her to misreach and bump herself.
visibleTrigger: Kiko raises hand, mirror's hand raises a second later.
whyThisObject: Mirror's function is immediate visual feedback; delay makes self-grooming impossible.
goalRuleRelationship: The action to style hair is based on outdated visual, so hands miss, preventing fixing.
frameZeroDescription: Kiko stands before mirror, her hand raised, but the reflection's hand is still lowered; a ghostly lag overlay is visible.
Beat 1
  Action: Kiko moves slowly, watching the mirror to adjust to delay.
  Visible consequence: The mirror's delay increases to three seconds, and her reflection starts combing hair that Kiko hasn't touched yet.
  New information: The lag can grow when she tries to compensate.
Beat 2
  Action: Kiko touches her hair while looking down, ignoring the mirror.
  Visible consequence: The mirror shows her reflection looking up and smiling, then her own head tilts involuntarily to match.
  New information: The delayed reflection can control her current actions, not just display.
Beat 3
  Action: Kiko covers the mirror with a towel.
  Visible consequence: The covered mirror continues to reflect, showing a delayed image on the towel's surface like a screen.
  New information: The reflection can project onto coverings.
Beat 4
  Action: Kiko turns the mirror around to face the wall.
  Visible consequence: The back of the mirror shows a live feed of Kiko from a second ago on the wall, and she sees herself walking backward.
  New information: The mirror's delay effect transfers to the wall surface.
Beat 5
  Action: Kiko stands perfectly still until the reflection catches up.
  Visible consequence: The reflection catches up and then freezes, while Kiko's own image splits into two identical Kikos in the room.
  New information: Synchronizing the delay creates a duplicate Kiko, solving the grooming by having the duplicate fix her hair.
finalPayoff: The reflection catches up and then freezes, while Kiko's own image splits into two identical Kikos in the room.
suggestedPayoffFamily: surreal resolution / duplication
loopPotential: The duplicate Kiko may also have a delayed reflection, causing infinite twins.
environment: Bathroom
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Mirror's real-time reflection is subverted by lag; visually clear when movements don't match.

B. NORMALIZED / DETERMINISTIC INTERPRETATION

33. raw-033 — Swapping Stairs
Disposition: REVISION_REQUIRED | Eligibility: INELIGIBLE | SFE: 25 | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-033
title: Swapping Stairs
protagonistSuggestion: Mimi
everydayGoal: Walk upstairs to bedroom
primaryObject: Stairs
normalExpectation: Stairs have uniform riser heights, easy to climb.
impossibleRule: The stair heights rearrange each step you take: the step you are about to step on moves up or down, alternating between 1 inch and 3 feet, causing missteps.
visibleTrigger: Mimi steps on first stair; the second stair rises to waist height.
whyThisObject: Stairs are meant to offer predictable vertical increments; randomizing heights makes climbing impossible without tripping.
goalRuleRelationship: The action of climbing is undermined by changing step dimensions, preventing reaching top.
frameZeroDescription: Mimi is mid-climb, one foot on a low step, the next step has risen to her waist, while another step behind has sunk to floor level.
Beat 1
  Action: Mimi tries to step over the high step onto the next.
  Visible consequence: The high step sinks as she lifts her leg, causing her to stomp down hard and stumble.
  New information: The stairs anticipate her movement and adjust to keep her off balance.
Beat 2
  Action: Mimi crawls on hands and knees to distribute weight.
  Visible consequence: The steps flatten to a ramp, then tilt sideways, sliding her toward the wall.
  New information: The staircase can morph into different configurations.
Beat 3
  Action: Mimi holds the railing and leaps up two steps.
  Visible consequence: The steps she skipped fold up like a ladder, pinning her leg briefly, and the next flight appears as a spiral.
  New information: Skipping steps causes them to fold into obstacles.
Beat 4
  Action: Mimi backs down and tries the escalator-like conveyor setting (if any).
  Visible consequence: The stairs start moving like an escalator but in reverse, taking her back to the ground floor.
  New information: The staircase has a direction setting that always opposes her desired direction.
Beat 5
  Action: Mimi gives up and sits on the bottom step.
  Visible consequence: The entire staircase folds into a flat ramp, and Mimi slides gently up to the bedroom door without using stairs.
  New information: The stairs become a ramp when not climbed, allowing effortless travel.
finalPayoff: The entire staircase folds into a flat ramp, and Mimi slides gently up to the bedroom door without using stairs.
suggestedPayoffFamily: absurd success after abandon
loopPotential: If Mimi tries to use the ramp, it might revert to chaotic stairs.
environment: House staircase
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Stairs' predictable steps are randomized in height; visually clear and directly blocks climbing.

35. raw-035 — Relocking Key
Disposition: MALFORMED | Eligibility: NOT_EVALUATED | SFE: None | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-035
title: Relocking Key
protagonistSuggestion: Arda
everydayGoal: Unlock front door
primaryObject: Key
normalExpectation: Turning key unlocks door so it opens.
impossibleRule: The key unlocks the door for exactly one second, then the door locks itself and spins the key back to locked; any attempt to open during that second causes the door to lock harder.
visibleTrigger: Arda turns key, hears click, but when he pushes, the door relocks and key spins back.
whyThisObject: Key is meant to disengage lock; if the lock re-engages instantly, unlocking is futile.
goalRuleRelationship: The action to unlock door results in immediate relocking, preventing entry.
frameZeroDescription: Arda stands at front door with key in lock, door visibly ajar for an instant, but the deadbolt is sliding back into locked position.
Beat 1
  Action: Arda turns key and immediately kicks door open.
  Visible consequence: The door opens a crack, then slams shut with such force that the key snaps in the lock.
  New information: Force against the door strengthens the relocking.
Beat 2
  Action: Arda turns key and holds it in the unlocked position, waiting.
  Visible consequence: The lock cylinder starts rotating on its own, spinning the key and twisting Arda's wrist.
  New information: The lock can overpower the key, turning it back.
Beat 3
  Action: Arda calls Mimi to turn the key from inside while he pushes.
  Visible consequence: The key turns, the door unlocks, but then a chain lock appears and locks itself across the door.
  New information: The lock can generate additional locking mechanisms to stay shut.
Beat 4
  Action: Arda removes the key and tries to pick the lock with a credit card.
  Visible consequence: The card is swallowed by the lock and spit out as a metal key that unlocks a different door across the street.
  New information: The lock redirects unlocking attempts to other doors, not this one.
Beat 5
  Action: Arda gives up and walks around to the back door.
  Visible consequence: The front door unlocks itself and swings open, then locks Arda inside a small porch as he steps through.
  New information: The door only opens when no one is trying, and then traps the person.
finalPayoff: The front door unlocks itself and swings open, then locks Arda inside a small porch as he steps through.
suggestedPayoffFamily: goal reached but new problem / self-trapping
loopPotential: Any future unlock attempt will trigger relocking; the door may lock him in or out unpredictably.
environment: Front door of house
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Key's unlocking function is reversed by instant relocking; visually clear with bolt sliding back.

37. raw-037 — Split Garbage Bag
Disposition: REVISION_REQUIRED | Eligibility: INELIGIBLE | SFE: 44 | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-037
title: Split Garbage Bag
protagonistSuggestion: Opa
everydayGoal: Take out the trash
primaryObject: Garbage bag
normalExpectation: Bag holds trash when tied and lifted.
impossibleRule: The bag remains intact in the can, but the moment you lift it, the seams split and trash falls back into the can; when you put bag back, it seals.
visibleTrigger: Opa lifts tied bag; bottom splits, trash drops.
whyThisObject: Garbage bag's containment is supposed to be stable under load; conditional splitting prevents removal.
goalRuleRelationship: The action to remove trash causes the bag to fail, so trash never leaves the can.
frameZeroDescription: Opa holds the tied top of a garbage bag, but the bottom is wide open, trash spilling back into the can.
Beat 1
  Action: Opa double-bags the trash with two bags.
  Visible consequence: The outer bag splits, the inner bag holds, but then the inner bag's top unknots and trash flies out like a fountain.
  New information: Multiple bags shift the failure point rather than preventing it.
Beat 2
  Action: Opa uses a box instead of a bag, tipping the trash into it.
  Visible consequence: The box's bottom opens when Opa lifts it, dumping trash back into the can.
  New information: The rule applies to any container lifted with trash, not just bags.
Beat 3
  Action: Opa scoops trash directly with a dustpan and carries it out.
  Visible consequence: The dustpan flips upside down just before he reaches the door, scattering trash.
  New information: The anti-lifting rule extends to dustpans.
Beat 4
  Action: Opa drags the entire trash can to the curb, not lifting anything.
  Visible consequence: The can's wheels spin off, and the can anchors itself to the floor, refusing to move.
  New information: Moving trash without lifting is also blocked; the can self-anchors.
Beat 5
  Action: Opa gives up and leaves the trash.
  Visible consequence: The bag climbs out of the can, stretches over the trash, and seals it perfectly, then levitates to the curb.
  New information: The trash removes itself only when Opa stops trying.
finalPayoff: The bag climbs out of the can, stretches over the trash, and seals it perfectly, then levitates to the curb.
suggestedPayoffFamily: goal achieved by object after abandon
loopPotential: If Opa tries to help next time, the bag will refuse again.
environment: Kitchen and curb
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Garbage bag's containment fails on lift; intuitive because you see bottom split and trash fall back.

38. raw-038 — Disappearing Ink Pen
Disposition: REJECTED | Eligibility: INELIGIBLE | SFE: 18 | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-038
title: Disappearing Ink Pen
protagonistSuggestion: Kiko
everydayGoal: Write a shopping list
primaryObject: Pen
normalExpectation: Ink marks stay on paper.
impossibleRule: The pen writes visible ink, but the lines evaporate after exactly one second, leaving blank paper; when the pen is capped, the marks reappear on the paper in reverse order.
visibleTrigger: Kiko writes 'milk', the word fades as she watches.
whyThisObject: Pen's function is to leave permanent marks; disappearing ink makes writing useless.
goalRuleRelationship: The action of writing list produces only temporary marks, so the list cannot be read later.
frameZeroDescription: Kiko holds a pen over a notepad; the word 'milk' is half-faded, the tail already invisible.
Beat 1
  Action: Kiko writes quickly and photographs the list before it fades.
  Visible consequence: The photo also fades, and the phone's screen becomes blank where the list was.
  New information: The disappearance transfers to any capture of the writing.
Beat 2
  Action: Kiko presses hard to dent paper so impressions remain.
  Visible consequence: The indentations flatten immediately, and the paper becomes smooth.
  New information: The paper self-heals indents, erasing physical evidence.
Beat 3
  Action: Kiko writes with a different pen (pencil).
  Visible consequence: The pencil marks also vanish, and the pencil itself disappears while writing.
  New information: The disappearing rule applies to all writing instruments, including the tool.
Beat 4
  Action: Kiko caps the pen and waits for marks to reappear.
  Visible consequence: The marks reappear in reverse order, forming a jumbled list that reads backwards from bottom to top.
  New information: Capping the pen reverses time for the ink, but reverses the sequence.
Beat 5
  Action: Kiko reads the reversed list and heads to the store.
  Visible consequence: The list on paper rearranges to normal as she shops, but every item she picks up vanishes from the cart.
  New information: The disappearing effect now applies to the actual items on the list, causing them to vanish when selected.
finalPayoff: The list on paper rearranges to normal as she shops, but every item she picks up vanishes from the cart.
suggestedPayoffFamily: property transfer to physical items
loopPotential: Any future list will cause items to disappear, making shopping impossible.
environment: Kitchen table and grocery store
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Pen's permanence is inverted into temporary ink; visually clear as words fade.

39. raw-039 — Smearing Eraser
Disposition: REVISION_REQUIRED | Eligibility: INELIGIBLE | SFE: 26 | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-039
title: Smearing Eraser
protagonistSuggestion: Mimi
everydayGoal: Erase a pencil mistake
primaryObject: Eraser
normalExpectation: Eraser lifts graphite from paper.
impossibleRule: Rubbing eraser spreads graphite into a larger smudge, and the smudge darkens into a sticky tar that stains fingers and paper.
visibleTrigger: Mimi erases a line; it widens into a gray smear.
whyThisObject: Eraser is designed to remove marks; if it smears, correcting mistakes is impossible.
goalRuleRelationship: The action to erase makes the mistake bigger and messier, preventing correction.
frameZeroDescription: Mimi holds an eraser on a paper; the erased area has turned into a dark smudge spreading outward.
Beat 1
  Action: Mimi uses a clean corner of the eraser, pressing lightly.
  Visible consequence: The smudge grows larger and darker, and the eraser corner becomes black.
  New information: The eraser absorbs graphite but redeposits it as tar.
Beat 2
  Action: Mimi blows away the eraser shavings.
  Visible consequence: The shavings fly up and stick to the paper, creating a swarm of black dots.
  New information: The tar-like graphite is airborne and attaches.
Beat 3
  Action: Mimi tries to wipe the smudge with a tissue.
  Visible consequence: The tissue sticks to the tar, then wads up and becomes a permanent black blob on the page.
  New information: Once smudged, the graphite becomes adhesive and traps cleaning tools.
Beat 4
  Action: Mimi uses a white correction tape over the smudge.
  Visible consequence: The tape turns black and peels off, pulling a layer of paper with it, leaving a hole.
  New information: The smudge corrodes any covering.
Beat 5
  Action: Mimi gives up and closes the notebook.
  Visible consequence: The smudge spreads to the opposite page, then shrinks into a perfect drawing of a cat, no longer a mistake.
  New information: When abandoned, the smudge transforms into a deliberate artwork, but not the intended correction.
finalPayoff: The smudge spreads to the opposite page, then shrinks into a perfect drawing of a cat, no longer a mistake.
suggestedPayoffFamily: absurd transformation / false success
loopPotential: If Mimi tries to replicate the cat, it may smear again.
environment: Study desk
dialogueNeed: NONE

40. raw-040 — Opposite-Side Butter Knife
Disposition: MALFORMED | Eligibility: NOT_EVALUATED | SFE: None | Ranking: None

A. PROVIDER RAW IDEA
ideaId: raw-040
title: Opposite-Side Butter Knife
protagonistSuggestion: Arda
everydayGoal: Butter a slice of bread
primaryObject: Butter knife
normalExpectation: Spreading butter with knife covers the top surface.
impossibleRule: The knife transfers butter to the opposite side of the bread (bottom) regardless of which side you spread; when you flip the bread, the butter flips back to the original top, leaving bottom clean.
visibleTrigger: Arda spreads butter on top; the top remains clean, but butter oozes out from the bottom.
whyThisObject: Knife's function is to apply spread to the intended surface; transferring to opposite side prevents the usual toast.
goalRuleRelationship: The action to butter one side results in butter on the other, so the intended side never gets butter.
frameZeroDescription: Arda holds a bread slice with knife mid-spread on top, but a thick layer of butter is visibly dripping from the underside.
Beat 1
  Action: Arda flips the bread over and butters the now-top side, which is currently buttered bottom.
  Visible consequence: The butter on the bottom jumps to the new top, leaving both sides alternately buttered and unbuttered in a loop.
  New information: Flipping only relocates the butter, not both sides buttered.
Beat 2
  Action: Arda holds the bread vertically and spreads on the edge.
  Visible consequence: Butter appears on the far edge, not the edge he's spreading, and drips onto his hand.
  New information: The knife transfers spread to the antipodal point on the bread.
Beat 3
  Action: Arda uses a different knife, a spoon.
  Visible consequence: The butter still ends up on the opposite side, and the spoon starts spinning, flinging butter across the kitchen.
  New information: The rule is tied to the act of spreading, not the knife.
Beat 4
  Action: Arda butters both sides at once using two knives.
  Visible consequence: Both knives transfer butter to each other's faces, leaving the bread dry and the knives coated.
  New information: Simultaneous spreading causes cross-transfer between tools.
Beat 5
  Action: Arda gives up and eats the bread plain.
  Visible consequence: The butter from the underside levitates and lands on his nose, then the bread flips and butters itself perfectly on both sides.
  New information: The bread can butter itself only when Arda stops trying, achieving the goal but with a mess.
finalPayoff: The butter from the underside levitates and lands on his nose, then the bread flips and butters itself perfectly on both sides.
suggestedPayoffFamily: role reversal / object self-buttering
loopPotential: Any future buttering attempt will cause butter on wrong side until he stops, then it self-corrects.
environment: Kitchen counter
dialogueNeed: NONE
durationSeconds: 15
providerRationale: Knife's spreading action is spatially inverted to opposite side; visually clear with butter dripping from bottom.

B. NORMALIZED / DETERMINISTIC INTERPRETATION
Not normalized.
